# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

Exact request: `POST /api/explore` with `{"text":"[89Z.r]-DFO-daratumumab"}`.

**Actual:** HTTP 200, centre **DARATUMUMAB / CHEMBL1743007**, matched alias **`89zr-dfo-daratumumab`**, **2 live trials**, and no Refused entity or refusal notice. Trial and sponsor QA each return **2 originator evidence rows**. `89Z_r-dfo-daratumumab` also leaks. Appending `, osimertinib` still selects DARATUMUMAB. [Independent HTTP](root_checks/independent_m12_http.json), [stage comparison](attacks/focused_results.json).

**Controls:** `89Zr-dfo-daratumumab` and `[89Zr]-DFO-daratumumab` refuse, with zero trial/sponsor evidence. Bare `daratumumab` correctly maps to CHEMBL1743007. Candidate and refused control have the **same canonical key `89zrdfodaratumumab`**, which the index associates with a single AACT synonym for DARATUMUMAB. Thus this is a normalized spelling of the repository's own refused tracer alias, not an unsupported drug guess. [Dictionary/predicate evidence](attacks/dictionary_evidence.json), [independent adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md).

| Stage | Result for `[89Z.r]-DFO-daratumumab` |
|---|---|
| Raw resolver | `CONTAINED_ALIAS` to CHEMBL1743007; no blocked component (underscore variant: `ALIAS`) |
| Resolver veto | `{}` |
| Item linker | Drug DARATUMUMAB; no Refused |
| Whole-name shortcut | `None`; it is not overriding an existing refusal |
| Public linker | Drug DARATUMUMAB; no Refused |
| Map / QA | Originator centre; trial/sponsor evidence 2/2 |

The causal chain is reproducible in the current code:

1. Positive lookup discards punctuation and consumes the full stored alias (`src/query.py:404-417`; `src/textrules.py:339-346`).
2. `_alnum_words` instead produces `['89Z', 'r', 'DFO', 'daratumumab']` (`src/query.py:111-113`). Inside-name isotope checks only inspect one/two-word runs joined by spaces (`src/query.py:258,278-280`). Neither `89Z` nor `89Z r` passes `is_isotope_label`, although glued `89Zr` does (`src/textrules.py:289-298`).
3. The latest glued-neighbour checks inspect words **outside** the consumed alias; there are none containing the isotope (`src/query.py:264-277`).
4. The latest isotope-plus-same-drug-key rule recognizes prefix `89zr`, but requires remainder `dfodaratumumab` to be another same-drug dictionary key (`src/query.py:282-286`). It has no index entry. Bare `daratumumab` does, but the intervening `DFO` remains.
5. The item-wide and raw resolver guards do not reassemble the split element symbol (`src/query.py:125-141`; `src/textrules.py:303-313`; `src/normalize.py:740-745`). `Engine.explore` then selects the surviving positive hit (`src/app.py:87-95`).

**Required outcome:** equivalent punctuation accepted by positive lookup must preserve the refused tracer identity inside a matched stored name, including intervening chelator text. Refusal must not depend on the isotope-stripped remainder being an independently stored alias. Existing lone-name and accepted over-refusal controls should remain explicit. This audit makes no implementation change.

The exact HEAD^ query module also leaks against the same current app/rules/database. **This is an inherited hole, not a regression attributed to this commit.** Only query.py is swapped in that counterfactual; it is not a reconstructed old deployment. [Independent current/previous checks](db_ui/adjudication.md).

| M11 supplementary, previously unpromoted `trastuzumab labelled with 89Zr`, `trastuzumab labeled with 89Zr`, `89Zr labeled trastuzumab`, `89Zr conjugated trastuzumab` | **NOT FIXED** — same originator maps as m11; not counted as a newly introduced defect or a separate blocker. [Reused original script](root_checks/fresh_probe.json), [current/previous adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md). |
| M11 supplementary `99MTc trastuzumab`, `[99MTc] trastuzumab` | **FIXED** — now refuse; bare trastuzumab control still maps. [Reused original script](root_checks/fresh_probe.json). |
| M11 punctuation-only cost overhead | **NOT FIXED as a cost concern** — comparable synthetic normalization overhead remains; no timeout or data error. [Same-data comparison](cost/normalization_cost.json). |

## Cost and nonblocking findings

Historical workloads were rerun sequentially after the other CPU-heavy review jobs finished. Unrelated host activity is uncontrolled; timings are observations, not service-level guarantees or proof of global linear complexity. Query-module counterfactuals use the same current database and remaining modules. [Execution](cost/execution_isolated.json), [reuse manifest](reuse_manifest_root.json).

| Reproduction | Current observation / disposition |
|---|---|
| M5-S3 cubic scan, 250 plain tokens | **FIXED for exact old workload:** 2,224 calls per predicate / 33,880 aggregate characters versus old 31,625 / 10,479,750. [Instrumentation](cost/perf_current.json). |
| Original 999-character plain-answer timeout | **FIXED:** check HTTP 200 in **0.22613 s**; ordinary follower **0.03572 s**. [HTTP](cost/perf_check_http.json). |
| M6/M7 repeated 2,000-piece input `';'.join(['x']*2000)` | **FIXED for exact workload:** current direct-link median **0.16853 s**, historical `9bd7cb4` **2.54663 s**; HTTP check **0.47434 s**, queued EGFR **0.39348 s**, both 200. [Comparison](cost/fresh_cost_comparison.json), [HTTP](cost/fresh_cost_http.json). |
| M8-S1 4,097th cache entry | **FIXED:** 82/82 fill requests create 4,096 distinct pieces; first osimertinib at rollover and retry both HTTP200/CHEMBL3353410/79 trials. Original 7,511-key sweep also completes. [HTTP](cost/cache_boundary_http.json). |
| M10/M11 400 distinct drug-bearing pieces, `';'.join(f'isa x{i:04d}' for i in range(400))` | **NOT FIXED**, inherited cost concern: cold check **3.41276 s**, queued EGFR **3.34385 s**, warm check **0.70656 s**. Resolver calls **400 cold / 0 warm**. [HTTP](cost/isolated_unique_cost_http.json), [instrumentation](cost/unique_piece_cost.json). |
| M11 Unicode expansion, answer `'\ufdfa'*4000` | **NOT FIXED:** 4,000 input characters expand to 72,000 at NFKC; check **4.13419 s**, queued EGFR **4.08618 s**, warm **4.16130 s**, all 200. [HTTP](cost/isolated_unicode_cost_http.json). |
| New repeated matches in one item, `' '.join(['isa']*1000)` (3,999 characters) | Nonblocking measured overhead: direct-link three-sample median **1.05735 s** versus HEAD^ **0.63607 s**. At 499 characters: **0.03516 / 0.03013 s**. No failure. [Inputs/comparison](cost/normalization_cost.json). |
| 3,999-character punctuation workload | Current / HEAD^ direct-link medians **0.34400 / 0.37192 s**. Cached ordinary osimertinib **0.000151 / 0.000119 s**. [Comparison](cost/normalization_cost.json). |
| Disease / ordinary concurrency | Disease: 2,774 Trials + 300 Targets, 30 per list, **18,575 bytes / 77.053 ms**. 24 requests /12 workers: all expected HTTP200 shapes, **842.766 ms** total. These ordinary samples overlap early audit work. [HTTP](root_checks/http_results.json). |

`engine.lock` serializes engine operations (`src/app.py:247-263`), so expensive legal `/api/check` answers delay maps. The 3,999/4,000-character stresses are direct-link or **answer-field** cases; `/api/explore` correctly rejects text over 500 characters. Limits precede normalization (`src/app.py:239-243,258-263`; `src/query.py:116-117,173-177`). No deadlock or invented graph value was observed.

**M12-S1 — new stored-alias over-refusal, nonblocking.** `rad001c` / `RAD001C` now refuses instead of selecting **EVEROLIMUS / CHEMBL1908360**, whereas the exact HEAD^ query module selects it. This is a stored AACT synonym. The new trailing-key rule divides `rad001c` into same-drug key `rad` and isotope-shaped `001c` (`src/query.py:282-286`; `src/textrules.py:289-298`). Raw resolver still returns ALIAS; no wrong centre is drawn. `rad`, `RAD001`, EVEROLIMUS and its ID still work. [Current/previous proof](names/new_alias_regression.json), [independent HTTP](root_checks/independent_m12_http.json).

The 7,511-key sweep has **70** previous-method positives without a current Drug, versus m11's 69; **`rad001c` is the sole additional loss**. Many existing losses are intended tracer/product refusals, so 70 is not a defect count. The explicitly accepted M5-S2, hydroxyethyl rapamycin, `Trastuzumab--vcmmae conjugate`, `9-amino-20(s)-camptothecin`, `il-6ra` and `TAK-659F` refusals remain nonblockers. [Loss-set comparison](names/new_alias_losses.json).

Supplementary attacks are recorded without inflating the blocker count: `[89Z.r]-labelled trastuzumab` leaks with 12/12 trial/sponsor rows while `[89Zr]-labelled trastuzumab` refuses; ordinary labelled-distance forms were already exercised and left unpromoted in m11. Punctuated lexical qualifiers and internal-isotope forms on **already labelled** LUTETIUM OXODOTREOTIDE LU-177 are not additional originator blocker claims. [Attack assessment](attacks/FINDINGS.md), [independent adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md). Existing blank-submit, unsupported-name, prefixed comma-alias and failure-closed Infinity/parser limitations are retained in the historical tables.

## Standards axis

**Zero documented-standards findings.** Independent review examined `git diff bfcb83d...HEAD` and the latest query/tests patch. No repository AGENTS.md/CONTRIBUTING/coding-standard source was found. The user-supplied evidence-based, no-tracked-edit and m12-only requirements govern this audit. KOA Studio / KhOpenApi original-versus-modified-code comparison is conditional on application changes; no such changes were made. [Independent standards assessment](db_ui/FINDINGS.md).

## Spec axis

**One P1 blocker, M12-B1.** A normalized spelling of the repository's refused tracer alias maps to its originator, with every refusal stage missing it. The exact m11 repairs work, but their same-drug remainder requirement leaves chelator-bearing aliases exposed. Stored-edge, stored-value, direction, cut-notice, no-model and exercised UI safety/state checks pass. Correct relationships around a wrongly chosen centre do not satisfy the refusal guarantee. Accepted over-refusals and cost observations do not determine the verdict. [Independent specification assessment](attacks/FINDINGS.md), [adjudication](db_ui/adjudication.md).

## Integrity and reproduction

Snapshot: **7 retired nodes, 142 retired edges, zero real self-loops and zero live edges with retired endpoints**. All six live node types and all 22 existing group combinations were exercised. Self-loop/unknown-type behavior was tested with an isolated in-memory fixture. [Snapshot](db_ui/db_snapshot_facts.json), [fixture](db_ui/self_loop_unknown.json).

`Engine.ask` uses `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:180-181`). Explore follows SELECT/read paths; mutation fixtures use memory copies. The application does not globally enforce a read-only SQLite handle, so this is an executed-path/source/hash result, not a database-permission guarantee. Final verification checks **3,731 tracked-file SHA-256 hashes**, HEAD, tracked/staged diffs, DB bytes, report links and owned-port cleanup. [Initial snapshot](integrity_before.json), [final integrity](integrity_final.json). DB SHA-256: `7341c5a7089731f67356da5a588b269d4b69e8e6c4c054acf19aa4ef2837b529`. Existing m12 brief/log files were preserved.

Run from repository root with bytecode disabled and TMP/TEMP/TMPDIR inside the corresponding m12 directory. Shared-port scripts must remain sequential; run benchmarks after other review work ends.

FILE audit_evidence_claims\m12\setup_root.py 
 """Snapshot tracked bytes and adapt previous audit runners without changing inputs."""
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib, json, subprocess
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT=OUT.parents[1]
tracked=subprocess.check_output(['git','ls-files','-z'],cwd=ROOT).decode('utf8').split('\0')
initial={'head':subprocess.check_output(['git','rev-parse','HEAD'],cwd=ROOT).decode().strip(),
 'tracked_sha256':{p:hashlib.sha256((ROOT/p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in tracked if p},
 'status':subprocess.check_output(['git','status','--porcelain'],cwd=ROOT).decode(),
 'db_sha256':hashlib.sha256((ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite').read_bytes()).hexdigest()}
(OUT/'integrity_before.json').write_text(json.dumps(initial,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
manifest=[]
for sub in ['root_checks','cost']:
 dest=OUT/sub
 (dest/'tmp').mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)
 for src in sorted((ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m11'/sub).glob('*.py')):
  if src.name in ('setup.py','build_report.py'):continue
  code=src.read_text(encoding='utf8')
  changes=[]
  for old,new in [('8897','8917'),('8898','8918'),('8899','8919'),('8894','8914')]:
   if old in code:code=code.replace(old,new);changes.append(f'port {old} -> {new}')
  if 'audit_evidence_claims/m11/' in code:
   raise RuntimeError('Review explicit round path before adapting: '+str(src))
  (dest/src.name).write_text(code,encoding='utf8')
  manifest.append({'source':src.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),'copy':(dest/src.name).relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),
   'sha256':hashlib.sha256(src.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'changes':changes,'output_root':'__file__ relative m12; historical inputs unchanged'})
(OUT/'reuse_manifest_root.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps({'tracked':len(initial['tracked_sha256']),'scripts':len(manifest),'head':initial['head']}))


FILE audit_evidence_claims\m12\finalize_integrity.py 
 """Final byte, path, lifecycle and report-link checks; never edit application files."""
from pathlib import Path
import datetime,hashlib,json,re,socket,subprocess
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent;ROOT=OUT.parents[1]
before=json.loads((OUT/'integrity_before.json').read_text(encoding='utf8'))
def git(*args):
 p=subprocess.run(['git',*args],cwd=ROOT,capture_output=True)
 return dict(code=p.returncode,stdout=p.stdout.decode('utf8','replace'),stderr=p.stderr.decode('utf8','replace'))
changed=[];missing=[]
for rel,digest in before['tracked_sha256'].items():
 p=ROOT/rel
 if not p.exists():missing.append(rel)
 elif hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()!=digest:changed.append(rel)
tracked=set(git('ls-files','-z')['stdout'].split('\0'))-{''}
ports={}
for port in [8914,8917,8918,8919,8920,8941,8962]:
 with socket.socket() as sock:
  sock.settimeout(.2);ports[str(port)]=sock.connect_ex(('127.0.0.1',port))!=0
report=(OUT/'REPORT.md').read_text(encoding='utf8')
links=re.findall(r'\]\(([^)]+)\)',report)
broken=[s for s in links if s!='integrity_final.json' and not (OUT/s.split('#',1)[0]).exists()]
db=hashlib.sha256((ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite').read_bytes()).hexdigest()
status=git('status','--short')
result=dict(time=datetime.datetime.now().astimezone().isoformat(),head_before=before['head'],head_after=git('rev-parse','HEAD')['stdout'].strip(),
 tracked_files_hashed=len(before['tracked_sha256']),changed_tracked_files=changed,missing_tracked_files=missing,
 new_tracked_paths=sorted(tracked-set(before['tracked_sha256'])),tracked_diff=git('diff','--exit-code'),staged_diff=git('diff','--cached','--exit-code'),
 git_status=status,initial_status=before['status'],database_sha256_before=before['db_sha256'],database_sha256_after=db,database_unchanged=db==before['db_sha256'],
 owned_ports_closed=ports,report_links_checked=len(links),broken_report_links=broken,report_final_line=report.rstrip().splitlines()[-1],
 report_sha256=hashlib.sha256((OUT/'REPORT.md').read_bytes()).hexdigest())
result['passed']=(not changed and not missing and not result['new_tracked_paths'] and result['head_before']==result['head_after']
 and result['tracked_diff']['code']==result['staged_diff']['code']==0 and result['database_unchanged'] and all(ports.values())
 and not broken and status['stdout']==before['status'] and result['report_final_line']=='VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED')
(OUT/'integrity_final.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps(result,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2))
assert result['passed'],'Final integrity verification failed'

FILE audit_evidence_claims\m12\root_checks\run_http.py 
 """Round 5 HTTP and QA regression checks; all output stays beside this file."""
import ast
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import re
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import types
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
PORT = 8917
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
os.environ["PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE"] = "1"
DB = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"

def git(*args):
    p = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
    return {"code": p.returncode, "stdout": p.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
            "stderr": p.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}

def post(path, payload):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    try:
        res = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
        res = e
    return {"status": res.status, "body": json.loads(res.read())}

before = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
initial = {"head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD"), "status": git("status", "--short"),
           "db_sha256": before}
(OUT / "initial_integrity.json").write_text(json.dumps(initial, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
with socket.socket() as s:
    assert s.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "audit port already occupied"

log = (OUT / "server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8")
proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)], cwd=ROOT,
                        env=os.environ.copy(), stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
                        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
try:
    for _ in range(100):
        if proc.poll() is not None:
            raise RuntimeError("server exited")
        try:
            urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
            break
        except OSError:
            time.sleep(.1)
    else:
        raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
    # Re-run every original HTTP case without executing a writer in m1.
    original = (ROOT / "audit_evidence_claims/m1/http_audit.py").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    adapted = original.replace("8876", str(PORT)).replace("bfcb83d", "514929c")
    (OUT / "http_audit.py").write_text(adapted.replace("OUT.parents[1]", "OUT.parents[2]"), encoding="utf-8")
    run = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", str(OUT / "http_audit.py")], cwd=ROOT,
                         capture_output=True, env={**os.environ, "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8"})
    (OUT / "http_stdout.txt").write_bytes(run.stdout + run.stderr)
    assert run.returncode == 0

    regression = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", str(OUT / "regression_http.py")],
        cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, env={**os.environ, "PYTHONIOENCODING":"utf-8"})
    (OUT / "regression_http_stdout.txt").write_bytes(regression.stdout + regression.stderr)
    assert regression.returncode == 0

    # Load the previous linker/answer module in memory; use the identical database.
    old = types.ModuleType("m2b_old_query")
    old.__file__ = str(ROOT / "src/query.py")
    sys.modules[old.__name__] = old
    code = subprocess.run(["git", "show", "bfcb83d:src/query.py"], cwd=ROOT,
                          capture_output=True, check=True).stdout.decode("utf-8")
    exec(compile(code, old.__file__, "exec"), old.__dict__)
    baseline = old.OncoKG()
    baseline.conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only=ON")
    html = (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    questions = json.loads(re.search(r"const EX2 = (\[.*?\]);", html, re.S).group(1))
    # Verify CLI/dashboard demos are covered by the page list, without running
    # their entrypoints (which write tracked report/dashboard artifacts).
    sources = {}
    for filename, variable in [("src/query.py", "questions"),
                               ("src/make_dashboard_data.py", "demo_questions")]:
        parsed = ast.parse((ROOT / filename).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        assignments = [n for n in ast.walk(parsed) if isinstance(n, ast.Assign)
                       and any(isinstance(t, ast.Name) and t.id == variable for t in n.targets)]
        value = assignments[-1].value
        if isinstance(value, ast.BoolOp):
            value = value.values[-1]
        sources[filename] = ast.literal_eval(value)
        assert set(sources[filename]) <= set(questions)
    answers = []
    for question in questions:
        previous = baseline.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()
        current = post("/api/ask", {"text": question})
        answers.append({"question": question, "http_status": current["status"],
                        "identical_to_bfcb83d": current["body"] == previous,
                        "current": current["body"], "previous": previous})
    baseline.conn.close()
    (OUT / "http_demo_results.json").write_text(json.dumps({"source_lists": sources,
        "answers": answers}, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
    print(json.dumps({"demo_questions": len(answers),
        "all_http_200": all(a["http_status"] == 200 for a in answers),
        "all_identical_to_bfcb83d": all(a["identical_to_bfcb83d"] for a in answers)}))
finally:
    proc.terminate()
    proc.wait(timeout=10)
    log.close()
    with socket.socket() as s:
        port_closed = s.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
    after = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
    (OUT / "http_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({"pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT,
        "server_exit": proc.returncode, "port_closed": port_closed,
        "database_unchanged": after == before, "db_sha256_before": before,
        "db_sha256_after": after}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

FILE audit_evidence_claims\m12\root_checks\run_verification.py 
 """Run the unmodified existing suite with scratch confined to this directory."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
import sys
import time

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
TMP = OUT / "tmp"
TMP.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
       "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP": str(TMP), "TEMP": str(TMP),
       "TMPDIR": str(TMP)}

def git(*args):
    result = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
    return {"code": result.returncode, "stdout": result.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
            "stderr": result.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}

files = ["src/app.py", "src/query.py", "web/try.html", "tests/test_app.py",
         "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"]
before = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
(OUT / "verification_initial.json").write_text(json.dumps({"head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD"),
    "status": git("status", "--short"), "hashes": before}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", "tests", "-q", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"]
start = time.perf_counter()
run = subprocess.run(cmd, cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True)
(OUT / "pytest.txt").write_bytes(run.stdout + run.stderr)
after = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
summary = {"command": "python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider",
           "environment": {k: env[k] for k in ("PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE", "PYTHONUTF8", "PYTHONIOENCODING", "TMP", "TEMP", "TMPDIR")},
           "exit_code": run.returncode, "wall_seconds": round(time.perf_counter() - start, 3),
           "hashes_unchanged": before == after, "hashes_after": after}
(OUT / "pytest_summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
print((run.stdout + run.stderr).decode("utf-8", "replace"))
print(json.dumps(summary, indent=2))
raise SystemExit(run.returncode)

FILE src/query.py
1: """Grounded question answering over the graph.
2: 
3: The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
4: question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
5: graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
6: dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.
7: 
8: Three properties the answering layer holds to:
9: 
10: * **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
11:   each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
12: * **The graph answers, not the model.** With a key, the language model is given
13:   the retrieved evidence and returns claims, not prose; each claim is checked
14:   against the graph and the answer is written from the accepted ones in the
15:   graph's own words (structured.py). With no key the evidence is rendered
16:   directly.
17: * **Nothing unsupported is shipped.** A claim the graph does not support is
18:   reported and left out. An unverifiable answer is worse than a short one.
19: """
20: from __future__ import annotations
21: 
22: import json
23: import os
24: import re
25: import sqlite3
26: import sys
27: import unicodedata
28: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
29: from pathlib import Path
30: 
31: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
32: import config as C
33: import normalize as N
34: import textrules as T
35: 
36: DB_PATH = C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite"
37: TRIAL_URL = "https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/{}"
38: 
39: 
40: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
41: # evidence
42: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
43: @dataclass
44: class Evidence:
45:     statement: str
46:     edge_id: str
47:     relation: str
48:     source: str
49:     source_record: str | None
50:     fetched_at: str | None
51:     confidence: float
52:     tier: str | None = None
53:     url: str | None = None
54:     detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
55: 
56:     def citation(self) -> str:
57:         base = self.source_record or self.source
58:         return f"[{base}]"
59: 
60: 
61: @dataclass
62: class Answer:
63:     question: str
64:     intent: str
65:     entities: list[dict]
66:     text: str
67:     evidence: list[Evidence]
68:     generated_by: str
69:     checks: dict
70: 
71:     def to_dict(self) -> dict:
72:         return {
73:             "question": self.question,
74:             "intent": self.intent,
75:             "entities": self.entities,
76:             "answer": self.text,
77:             "generated_by": self.generated_by,
78:             "checks": self.checks,
79:             "evidence": [
80:                 {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
81:                  "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
82:                  "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
83:                  "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
84:                 for e in self.evidence
85:             ],
86:         }
87: 
88: 
89: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
90: # engine
91: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
92: # Words that list separately named things; a list item ends at one. "with" is not among
93: # them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
94: # nanoparticles").
95: _JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
96:             "or/and", "and/or"}
97: 
98: 
99: 
100: # every Unicode dash (category Pd) and minus sign, read as a hyphen
101: _DASHES = "[" + "".join(re.escape(chr(c)) for c in range(0x110000)
102:                         if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd" or chr(c) in "\u2212\u207b\u208b") + "]"
103: 
104: 
105: def _plain(text: str) -> str:
106:     """Accents off, as the alias key does."""
107:     t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
108:     return unicodedata.normalize("NFC", "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c)))
109: 
110: 
111: def _alnum_words(text: str) -> list[str]:
112:     """Letters-and-digits words, with every other character a separator."""
113:     return re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(text))
114: 
115: 
116: def _fold(text: str) -> str:
117:     return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))
118: 
119: def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
120:     """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?
121: 
122:     The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
123:     biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
124:     work grows linearly with the item."""
125:     def hit(text: str) -> bool:
126:         # the alias key ignores punctuation, underscores and accents, so the guards also
127:         # see the text with those folded ("bevacizumab-(awwb)", "bevacizumab_awwb",
128:         # "bevácizumab-awwb")
129:         plain = _plain(text)
130:         # and with every separator (punctuation, underscore, spaces) read as one space,
131:         # so "generic-version of", "albumin - bound" meet the multiword patterns
132:         return any(T.is_distinct_product(v) or T.is_radiotracer(v)
133:                    for v in {text, plain, re.sub(r"(?:[^\w\s]|_)+", "-", plain).strip("-"),
134:                              " ".join(_alnum_words(plain))})
135: 
136:     if hit(" ".join(words)):
137:         return True
138:     if any(T.canonical_key(w) in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES for w in words):
139:         return True
140:     return any(hit(" ".join(words[i:j])) for i in range(len(words))
141:                for j in range(i + 1, min(len(words), i + 8) + 1))
142: 
143: class OncoKG:
144:     def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
145:                  resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
146:         self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
147:         self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
148:         resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
149:         self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
150:         self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
151:         self._resolve_cache: dict = {}
152:         self.disease = resolved["disease"]
153:         self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
154:                         if t.get("symbol")}
155:         # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
156:         # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
157:         self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
158:         for row in self.conn.execute(
159:                 "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
160:             self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
161:             self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
162:         # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
163:         for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
164:             if same in self.companies:
165:                 self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
166:         self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}
167: 
168:     # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
169:     def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
170:         # one spelling for every check: compatibility forms folded, every dash a hyphen
171:         question = _fold(question)
172:         blocked = self._resolver_blocked(question)
173:         found = self._link(question)
174:         whole = self._whole_name(question, blocked, found)
175:         if whole:
176:             return [whole]
177:         if not blocked:
178:             return found
179:         # a drug the resolver refuses in this text is refused here too, whatever the
180:         # splitting below made of it (the two must agree before anything is linked)
181:         out = [e for e in found if not (e["type"] == "Drug" and e["id"] in blocked)]
182:         for text in sorted({t for t in blocked.values()}):
183:             if not any(e["type"] == "Refused" and (text in e["label"] or e["label"] in text)
184:                        for e in out):
185:                 out.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text, "matched_text": text,
186:                             "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not the "
187:                                    "originator molecule"})
188:         return out
189: 
190:     def _resolver_blocked(self, text: str) -> dict:
191:         """Drug ids inside the pieces the intervention resolver refuses as a distinct
192:         product or a tracer, with the refused piece."""
193:         comps = []
194:         lone = set()
195:         pieces = {p.strip() for p in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001/|]", text) if p.strip()}
196:         for piece in pieces:                         # each distinct list piece once
197:             words = [w for w in re.split(r"[^\w\-]+", piece) if w]
198:             if not self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
199:                 continue                             # no drug in it: nothing to refuse
200:             got = self._resolve_cache.get(piece)
201:             if got is None:
202:                 try:
203:                     got = N.resolve_drug_string(piece, self.dic, self.index_keys)["components"]
204:                 except Exception:                    # never let a guard crash a lookup
205:                     got = []
206:                 if len(self._resolve_cache) >= 4096:
207:                     self._resolve_cache.clear()
208:                 self._resolve_cache[piece] = got
209:             if len(words) == 1 and T.canonical_key(piece) in self.dic["index"]:
210:                 lone.add(piece)                      # one stored name on its own ("566C")
211:             comps += got
212:         out: dict = {}
213:         accepted = {c.get("drug_id") for c in comps
214:                     if c.get("drug_id") and not str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED")}
215:         for c in comps:
216:             piece = c.get("component") or ""
217:             tracer = "radiotracer_guard" in (c.get("rules") or []) or T.is_radiotracer(piece)
218:             if not (c.get("tier") == "BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT"
219:                     or (str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED") and tracer)):
220:                 continue
221:             if piece in lone:
222:                 continue
223:             words = [w for w in re.split(r"[\s/|,;]+", piece) if w]
224:             words = [w.strip(" ?.!:()[]") or w for w in words]
225:             for a, b in self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
226:                 key = T.canonical_key(" ".join(words[a:b]))
227:                 for h in self.dic["index"].get(key, []):
228:                     # the same molecule named separately and accepted ("biosimilar
229:                     # anastrozole, Arimidex") is left to the list-item rule below
230:                     if h["drug_id"] not in accepted:
231:                         out[h["drug_id"]] = piece
232:         return out
233: 
234:     def _marked(self, key: str, did: str, tokens, seg, a: int, b: int):
235:         """The text that makes this drug name a distinct product or a tracer, if any.
236: 
237:         Checked on the same letters-and-digits form the name was looked up by: a known
238:         biosimilar suffix at the end of the name ("bevacizumab awwb", "bevacizumab_awwb")
239:         or as the next word, or an isotope label just before or after it ("[89.Zr]
240:         trastuzumab", "89:Zr trastuzumab")."""
241:         for sfx in T.BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES:
242:             stem = key[:-len(sfx)]
243:             if key.endswith(sfx) and stem and any(
244:                     h["drug_id"] == did for h in self.dic["index"].get(stem, [])):
245:                 return " ".join(tokens[a:b])
246:         same = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == seg[a]]
247:         before = [k for k in same if k < a]
248:         after = [k for k in same if k >= b]
249:         # words counted after normalisation, so punctuation never uses up the budget
250:         prev = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in before))[-2:]
251:         nxt = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in after))[:2]
252:         own = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[a:b]))
253:         lone = not before and not after      # the name is the whole list item
254:         # an isotope label anywhere in the name ("A33 124i"), or just before or after it
255:         # ("[89.Zr] trastuzumab", "trastuzumab 89 - Zr"); an isotope-shaped name counts
256:         # as a name only when it stands alone ("566C", "18f"), never as the substance
257:         # of a longer phrase ("18F fluorothymidine")
258:         inside = [own[i:j] for i in range(len(own)) for j in (i + 1, i + 2) if j <= len(own)]
259:         # and every run of the name's words glued as the key glues them, so a symbol
260:         # split by punctuation inside a stored alias is still an isotope ("[89Z.r]-DFO-")
261:         for i in range(len(own)):
262:             for j in range(i + 1, len(own) + 1):
263:                 glued = "".join(own[i:j])
264:                 if len(glued) > 6:
265:                     break
266:                 if j - i > 1:
267:                     inside.append([glued])
268:         if lone and T.is_isotope_label("".join(own)):
269:             inside = []                      # the whole item is one isotope-shaped name
270:         name = " ".join(tokens[a:b])
271:         # the neighbours glued back together the way the lookup key glues them, so a
272:         # suffix or an isotope spelt in pieces is still seen ("a - w w b", "[89Z.r]")
273:         nxt_all = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in after))[:8]
274:         prev_all = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in before))[-8:]
275:         for n in range(1, len(nxt_all) + 1):
276:             glued = "".join(nxt_all[:n]).lower()
277:             if glued in T.BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES or T.is_isotope_label(glued):
278:                 return f"{name} ({' '.join(nxt_all[:n])})"
279:             if len(glued) > 6:
280:                 break
281:         for n in range(1, len(prev_all) + 1):
282:             glued = "".join(prev_all[-n:]).lower()
283:             if T.is_isotope_label(glued):
284:                 return f"({' '.join(prev_all[-n:])}) {name}"
285:             if len(glued) > 6:
286:                 break
287:         for ws in inside + [prev[-1:], prev, nxt[:1], nxt]:
288:             if ws and T.is_isotope_label(" ".join(ws)):
289:                 return name if ws in inside else f"{name} ({' '.join(ws)})"
290:         # an isotope glued to the name itself ("89zr-trastuzumab" read through "[89Z.r]")
291:         for i in range(2, min(6, len(key))):
292:             for iso, rest in ((key[:i], key[i:]), (key[-i:], key[:-i])):
293:                 if rest and T.is_isotope_label(iso) and any(
294:                         h["drug_id"] == did for h in self.dic["index"].get(rest, [])):
295:                     return name
296:         return None
297: 
298:     def _whole_name(self, text: str, blocked: dict, found: list):
299:         """The whole input is exactly one stored drug name, letter for letter ("CP-358,774",
300:         "(E/Z)-AFATINIB"): read it whole rather than as the list its punctuation suggests.
301: 
302:         Only after every refusal has had its say: nothing the resolver or the list
303:         linker refused, and no product or tracer pattern in the name."""
304:         if blocked or any(e["type"] == "Refused" for e in found):
305:             return None
306:         name = text.strip(" ?!.")
307:         if T.is_distinct_product(name) or T.is_radiotracer(name):
308:             return None             # the list linker above has already applied the rest
309:         key = T.canonical_key(name)
310:         exact = [h for h in (self.dic["index"].get(key) or [])
311:                  if _fold(h["alias"]).strip(" ?!.").lower() == name.lower()]
312:         if not exact or len({h["drug_id"] for h in exact}) != 1:
313:             return None
314:         did = exact[0]["drug_id"]
315:         return {"type": "Drug", "id": did, "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
316:                 "matched_text": name, "matched_alias": exact[0]["alias"]}
317: 
318:     def _link(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
319:         found: list[dict] = []
320:         seen: set = set()
321:         tokens: list[str] = []
322:         clause: list[int] = []          # which list piece each token is in
323:         inner: list[bool] = []          # token inside parentheses
324:         ci, depth = 0, 0
325:         for piece in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001]", question):
326:             for raw in re.split(r"\s+", piece):
327:                 # a slash or bar, glued or free, ends the piece ("bevacizumab - awwb/
328:                 # osimertinib"); it must not hide a product suffix from the guards
329:                 for j, part in enumerate(re.split(r"[/|]", raw)):
330:                     if j:
331:                         ci += 1
332:                     if not part:
333:                         continue
334:                     if re.search(r"\w", part):
335:                         # edge punctuation and wrappers off ("bevacizumab-awwb?",
336:                         # "(bevacizumab-awwb)"); an isotope ("[89Zr]") is checked as a word
337:                         t = re.sub(r"^[^\w]+|[^\w]+$", "", part)
338:                     elif part in ("-", "\u2013", "\u2014", "+", "&"):
339:                         t = part            # "bevacizumab - awwb" needs its dash
340:                     else:
341:                         t = ""
342:                     if t:
343:                         tokens.append(t)
344:                         clause.append(ci)
345:                         inner.append(depth > 0 or part.startswith("("))
346:                     depth = max(0, depth + part.count("(") - part.count(")"))
347:             ci += 1
348: 
349:         # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too: "Mvasi", "liposomal
350:         # doxorubicin" or "generic version of bevacizumab" must never answer as the
351:         # originator molecule. The text is cut into list items at commas, semicolons
352:         # and listing words ("and", "or", "plus", ...). An item that names a distinct
353:         # product or a tracer anywhere has every drug in it refused: which drug a
354:         # qualifier describes is not guessed, so "osimertinib with generic version of
355:         # bevacizumab" refuses both, while "osimertinib and generic version of
356:         # bevacizumab" keeps osimertinib. Targets and companies in the item still link.
357:         seg: list[int] = []
358:         n = 0
359:         for k, t in enumerate(tokens):
360:             # "and" inside parentheses describes one product ("doxorubicin
361:             # (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)"); it does not list two
362:             if t.lower() in _JOINERS and not inner[k]:
363:                 n += 1
364:                 seg.append(-1)
365:                 continue
366:             if k and clause[k] != clause[k - 1]:
367:                 n += 1
368:             seg.append(n)
369:         refused: set[int] = {k for k, t in enumerate(tokens)
370:                              if T.is_distinct_product(t) or T.is_radiotracer(t)}
371:         drugs = self._drug_spans(tokens, {k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] < 0}, seg)
372:         dangling = None                 # a qualifier item with no drug, joined by "and"/"or"
373:         prev_end = -1
374:         for sid in sorted(set(seg) - {-1}):
375:             idx = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == sid]
376:             text = " ".join(tokens[k] for k in idx)
377:             joined = (dangling is not None and idx[0] > prev_end + 1
378:                       and all(seg[k] < 0 for k in range(prev_end + 1, idx[0]))
379:                       and clause[idx[0]] == clause[prev_end])
380:             prev_end = idx[-1]
381:             hit = [k for a, b in drugs if seg[a] == sid for k in range(a, b)]
382:             if _qualified([tokens[k] for k in idx]):
383:                 named = any(k in refused and (
384:                     T.canonical_key(tokens[k]) in self.dic["index"]
385:                     or tokens[k].lower() in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES)
386:                     for k in idx)
387:                 if not hit and not named:
388:                     # "liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin": a bare
389:                     # qualifier carries over a listing word to the next item's drug; a
390:                     # product named by itself ("Mvasi and bevacizumab") does not
391:                     dangling = text
392:                     continue
393:                 dangling = None
394:             elif joined and hit:
395:                 text = f"{dangling} ... {text}"
396:                 dangling = None
397:             else:
398:                 dangling = None if not joined else dangling
399:                 continue
400:             refused.update(hit)
401:             found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text,
402:                           "matched_text": text,
403:                           "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
404:                                  "the originator molecule"})
405: 
406:         matched: set[int] = set()
407:         for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
408:             for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
409:                 if (refused | matched) & set(range(i, i + size)):
410:                     continue
411:                 if size > 1 and (len(set(seg[i:i + size])) > 1 or min(seg[i:i + size]) < 0):
412:                     continue            # a name never spans two list items
413:                 window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
414:                 if len(window) < 3:
415:                     continue
416:                 key = T.canonical_key(window)
417: 
418:                 hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
419:                 # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
420:                 # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
421:                 # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
422:                 if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
423:                     hits = None
424:                 if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
425:                     did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
426:                     why = self._marked(key, did, tokens, seg, i, i + size)
427:                     if why:
428:                         # the name carries a product suffix or sits beside an isotope:
429:                         # refuse it, never fall back to the bare molecule
430:                         found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": why,
431:                                       "matched_text": why,
432:                                       "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
433:                                              "the originator molecule"})
434:                         refused.update(range(i, i + size))
435:                         continue
436:                     if ("Drug", did) not in seen:
437:                         seen.add(("Drug", did))
438:                         found.append({"type": "Drug", "id": did,
439:                                       "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
440:                                       "matched_text": window,

FILE src/app.py
1: """A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.
2: 
3:     python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765
4: 
5: A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
6: click to move on),
7: then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
8: how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
9: evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
10: claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
11: see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
12: the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
13: OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
14: pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
15: """
16: from __future__ import annotations
17: 
18: import json
19: import sqlite3
20: import sys
21: import threading
22: from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
23: from pathlib import Path
24: 
25: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
26: import config as C
27: import normalize as N
28: import textrules as T
29: from query import OncoKG
30: 
31: PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
32: PORT = 8765
33:

35: class Engine:
36:     def __init__(self):
37:         self.kg = OncoKG()
38:         # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
39:         # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
40:         self.kg.conn.close()
41:         self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
42:         self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
43:         self.lock = threading.Lock()
44:         self.dic = self.kg.dic
45:         self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
46:         target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
47:         self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)
48: 
49:     def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
50:         out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
51:         for c in out["components"]:
52:             d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
53:             if d:
54:                 c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
55:                              ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
56:                 c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
57:                                            for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
58:                 c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
59:                     "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
60:                     (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
61:                 par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
62:                 if par:
63:                     c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
64:                         "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
65:                         (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
66:             if c.get("candidates"):
67:                 c["candidate_names"] = [
68:                     (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
69:         return out
70: 
71:     def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
72:         """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.
73: 
74:         `text` is a node id or a name the question linker reads as one entity. A name
75:         the linker refuses (a biosimilar, a reformulation, a tracer) is reported, never
76:         mapped to the originator. Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the
77:         page states: trials by start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph,
78:         sponsors by trials they lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship
79:         keeps its role (lead sponsor or collaborator)."""
80:         conn = self.kg.conn
81:         also: list = []
82:         matched = None
83:         row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
84:                            (text,)).fetchone()
85:         if row:
86:             nid = row["id"]
87:         else:
88:             ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
89:             refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
90:             hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
91:             if not hits:
92:                 if refused:
93:                     return {"refused": refused[0]["label"], "why": refused[0]["why"]}
94:                 return {"not_found": text}
95:             nid, matched = hits[0]["id"], hits[0].get("matched_alias") or hits[0].get("matched_text")
96:             also = [{"id": e["id"], "type": e["type"], "label": e["label"]} for e in hits[1:]]
97:             for oid in hits[0].get("also_matching_ids") or []:   # one alias, several ids
98:                 lab = conn.execute("SELECT type, label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
99:                                    (oid,)).fetchone()
100:                 if lab and oid != nid:
101:                     also.append({"id": oid, "type": lab["type"], "label": lab["label"]})
102:             # the same words may also name an organisation or a target the linker
103:             # passed over because a drug alias came first ("Roche" is a folic acid alias)
104:             key = T.canonical_key(text)
105:             for cid in self.kg.companies.get(key, []):
106:                 if cid != nid and all(x.get("id") != cid for x in also):
107:                     lab = conn.execute("SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
108:                                        (cid,)).fetchone()
109:                     if lab:
110:                         also.append({"id": cid, "type": "Company", "label": lab["label"]})
111:             t = self.kg.targets.get(text.strip().lower())
112:             if t and t["target_id"] != nid:
113:                 also.append({"id": t["target_id"], "type": "Target", "label": t["symbol"]})
114:             also += [{"refused": e["label"]} for e in refused]
115:         node = conn.execute("SELECT id, type, label, props FROM node WHERE id=? "
116:                             "AND retired_at IS NULL", (nid,)).fetchone()
117:         if node is None:                              # a name can outlive its node
118:             return {"not_found": text}
119:         rows = conn.execute(
120:             "SELECT e.id AS eid, e.rel, e.src, e.props AS eprops, n.id, n.type, n.label, "
121:             "n.props AS nprops FROM edge e JOIN node n "
122:             "ON n.id = CASE WHEN e.src=? THEN e.dst ELSE e.src END "
123:             "WHERE (e.src=? OR e.dst=?) AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND n.retired_at IS NULL",
124:             (nid, nid, nid)).fetchall()
125:         drug_ids = list({r["id"] for r in rows if r["type"] == "Drug"} | {nid})
126:         trials = {}
127:         for i in range(0, len(drug_ids), 500):
128:             chunk = drug_ids[i:i + 500]
129:             trials.update(conn.execute(               # live trials only
130:                 "SELECT e.dst, COUNT(*) FROM edge e JOIN node t ON t.id = e.src "
131:                 "WHERE e.rel='EVALUATES' AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND t.retired_at IS NULL "
132:                 f"AND e.dst IN ({','.join('?' * len(chunk))}) GROUP BY e.dst", chunk).fetchall())
133:         groups: dict = {}
134:         for r in rows:
135:             ep, np_ = _obj(r["eprops"]), _obj(r["nprops"])
136:             item = {"id": r["id"], "type": r["type"], "label": r["label"], "edge_id": r["eid"]}
137:             role = ep.get("role") if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS" else None
138:             if r["type"] == "Drug":
139:                 item["trials"] = trials.get(r["id"], 0)
140:                 item["max_phase"] = np_.get("max_phase_overall")
141:             if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF":
142:                 n = ep.get("evidence_trials")
143:                 # a count the edge does not record stays unknown; 0 is only for sorting
144:                 item["evidence_trials"] = n if _number(n) else None
145:                 item["weight"] = n if _number(n) else 0
146:                 item["sponsor_class"] = ep.get("sponsor_class")
147:             elif r["type"] == "Trial":
148:                 item.update({k: np_.get(k) for k in ("phases", "status", "title", "start_date", "url")})
149:                 item["weight"] = str(np_.get("start_date") or "")
150:             elif r["type"] == "Drug":
151:                 item["weight"] = item["trials"]
152:             elif r["rel"] == "ASSOCIATED_WITH":
153:                 score = ep.get("association_score")
154:                 item["score"] = score if _number(score) else None
155:                 item["weight"] = item["score"] or 0
156:             else:
157:                 item["weight"] = 0
158:             if r["rel"] == "ACTS_ON":
159:                 item["action"] = ep.get("action_type")
160:             if role:
161:                 item["role"] = role
162:             key = (r["rel"], "out" if r["src"] == nid else "in", r["type"], role)
163:             groups.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
164:         out = []
165:         for (rel, direction, ntype, role), items in groups.items():
166:             items.sort(key=lambda x: x["label"] or "")              # names A to Z
167:             items.sort(key=lambda x: x["weight"], reverse=True)     # then by the stated weight
168:             out.append({"rel": rel, "dir": direction, "type": ntype, "role": role,
169:                         "total": len(items), "items": items[:per_group]})
170:         out.sort(key=lambda g: -g["total"])
171:         props = _obj(node["props"])
172:         return {"center": {"id": node["id"], "type": node["type"], "label": node["label"],
173:                            "matched_text": matched,
174:                            "trials": trials.get(node["id"], 0) if node["type"] == "Drug" else None,
175:                            "props": {k: props.get(k) for k in
176:                                      ("max_phase_overall", "drug_type", "phases", "status",
177:                                       "title", "url", "start_date") if props.get(k) is not None}},
178:                 "also": also, "groups": out}
179: 
180:     def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
181:         return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()
182: 
183:     def structured(self, question: str, payload: str) -> dict:
184:         """Check a structured answer (claims citing edges) and write the answer from it."""
185:         kg = self.kg
186:         entities = kg.link_entities(question)
187:         evidence = kg.retrieve(kg.classify(question, entities), entities)
188:         res = kg.verify_structured(payload, evidence)
189:         res["evidence"] = [{"edge_id": e.edge_id, "statement": e.statement} for e in evidence]
190:         return res
191: 
192:     def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
193:         """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
194:         kg = self.kg
195:         entities = kg.link_entities(question)
196:         intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
197:         evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
198:         checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
199:                                        vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
200:                                        linker=kg.link_entities)
201:         return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
202:                 "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
203:                               "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
204:                 "evidence_count": len(evidence)}
205: 
206: 
207: def _number(v) -> bool:
208:     return isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool) and v == v
209: 
210: 
211: def _obj(raw) -> dict:
212:     """Stored props as a dict; anything else (null, a list, broken JSON) as empty."""
213:     try:
214:         v = json.loads(raw) if raw else {}
215:     except ValueError:
216:         return {}
217:     return v if isinstance(v, dict) else {}
218: 
219: 
220: def make_handler(engine: Engine):
221:     class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
222:         def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
223:             self.send_response(code)
224:             self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
225:             self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
226:             self.end_headers()
227:             self.wfile.write(body)
228: 
229:         def do_GET(self):
230:             if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
231:                 self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
232:             else:
233:                 self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")
234: 
235:         def do_POST(self):
236:             try:
237:                 n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
238:                 body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
239:                 text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()
240:                 if len(text) > 500:                      # refused, not silently cut
241:                     raise ValueError("input is longer than 500 characters")
242:                 if not text:
243:                     raise ValueError("empty input")
244:                 if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check",
245:                                      "/api/structured", "/api/explore"):
246:                     return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
247:                 with engine.lock:
248:                     if self.path == "/api/explore":
249:                         res = engine.explore(text)
250:                     elif self.path == "/api/resolve":
251:                         res = engine.resolve(text)
252:                     elif self.path == "/api/ask":
253:                         res = engine.ask(text)
254:                     elif self.path == "/api/structured":
255:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:20000]
256:                         if not answer:
257:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
258:                         res = engine.structured(text, answer)
259:                     else:
260:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
261:                         if not answer:
262:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
263:                         res = engine.check(text, answer)
264:                 self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False,
265:                                            allow_nan=False).encode("utf-8"),
266:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
267:             except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
268:                 self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
269:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
270: 
271:         def log_message(self, *a):
272:             pass
273:     return Handler

275: 

FILE src/textrules.py
250: # "pembrolizumab-free regimen", "free of steroids": an absence written after
251: # the substance rather than before it
252: # ("free base" is a chemical form, not an absence)
253: RE_NEGATED_AFTER = re.compile(
254:     r"\b[A-Za-z0-9]+[\s\-]free\b(?![\s\-]*base)|\bfree\s+of\b", re.I)
255: 
256: 
257: def is_negated(text: str) -> bool:
258:     t = text or ""
259:     return bool(RE_NEGATED.match(t) or RE_NEGATED_AFTER.search(t))
260: 
261: # A placebo arm must never create a "this company administered this drug" edge.
262: RE_PLACEBO = re.compile(r"\bplacebo\b|\bsham\b|\bvehicle\s+control\b", re.I)
263: 
264: # Combination separators, longest first so "combined with" wins over "with".
265: RE_COMBO = re.compile(
266:     r"\s*(?:\+|&|,|、|，|/(?=\s*[A-Za-z])|\bcombined\s+with\b|"
267:     r"\bin\s+combination\s+with\b|\bcombination\s+with\b|\bplus\b|\band\b|\bor\b|"
268:     r"\bfollowed\s+by\b)\s*",
269:     re.I,
270: )
271: 
272: RE_PAREN = re.compile(r"\(([^()]*)\)|\[([^\[\]]*)\]")
273: 
274: # Development codes ("AZD9291", "BMS-986504", "PF-07224826", "TSC-204-A0201").
275: # One character apart is a different molecule, so these must never be matched
276: # approximately -- the usual string-similarity intuition is wrong here.
277: RE_DEV_CODE = re.compile(
278:     r"^[A-Za-z]{1,5}[\s\-]?\d{2,8}(?:[\s\-][A-Za-z0-9]{1,8})*$")
279: 
280: # Trailing lot/format suffixes that do not change the substance: "BMS-936558-01"
281: RE_DEV_CODE_SUFFIX = re.compile(r"^(?P<core>[A-Za-z]{1,5}[\s\-]?\d{3,8})[\s\-]0?\d{1,2}$")
282: RE_MULTISPACE = re.compile(r"\s+")
283: RE_PUNCT_EDGE = re.compile(r"^[\s\-–—,;:.、，。]+|[\s\-–—,;:.、，。]+$")
284: 
285: 
286: # Isotope labels on radiotracers: 18F, 68Ga, 99mTc, 177Lu, 64Cu, 89Zr, 131I.
287: # These look exactly like development codes but name an isotope, not a molecule,
288: # and the substance they label is usually not in a therapeutic vocabulary at all.
289: RE_ISOTOPE = re.compile(r"^\[?(\d{1,3}m?)[\s\-]?([A-Za-z]{1,2})\]?$")
290: ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS = {
291:     "f", "c", "n", "o", "ga", "cu", "zr", "i", "lu", "tc", "in", "y", "at",
292:     "ac", "pb", "re", "sm", "sr", "ra", "rb", "br", "h", "p", "s",
293: }
294: 
295: 
296: def is_isotope_label(text: str) -> bool:
297:     m = RE_ISOTOPE.match((text or "").strip())
298:     return bool(m and m.group(2).lower() in ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS)
299: 
300: 
301: # "[18F]F-AraG", "68Ga-DOTATATE": an isotope prefix marks the whole string as a
302: # radiolabelled tracer, whose unlabelled moiety is not the administered drug.
303: RE_RADIOTRACER = re.compile(
304:     r"(?:^|[\s\(\[])\[?\d{1,3}m?[\s\-]?[A-Za-z]{1,2}\]?\s*[-–]\s*\w"
305:     r"|(?:^|[\s\(])\[\d{1,3}m?[\s\-]?[A-Za-z]{1,2}\]\s*[A-Za-z]", re.I)
306: 
307: 
308: def is_radiotracer(text: str) -> bool:
309:     t = (text or "").strip()
310:     m = RE_RADIOTRACER.search(t)
311:     if not m:
312:         return False
313:     head = re.match(r"^[\s\(\[]*\[?(\d{1,3})m?[\s\-]?([A-Za-z]{1,2})\]?", t)
314:     return bool(head and head.group(2).lower() in ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS)
315: 
316: 
317: def is_dev_code(text: str) -> bool:
318:     """True for alphanumeric development codes, where fuzzy matching is unsafe."""
319:     t = (text or "").strip()
320:     return bool(RE_DEV_CODE.match(t))
321: 
322: 
323: def dev_code_core(text: str) -> str | None:
324:     """Strip a trailing lot/format number: 'BMS-936558-01' -> 'BMS-936558'."""
325:     m = RE_DEV_CODE_SUFFIX.match((text or "").strip())
326:     return m.group("core") if m else None
327: 
328: 
329: def _strip_words(text: str, words: list[str]) -> tuple[str, bool]:
330:     fired = False
331:     for w in sorted(words, key=len, reverse=True):
332:         pattern = re.compile(rf"\b{re.escape(w)}\b", re.I)
333:         if pattern.search(text):
334:             text = pattern.sub(" ", text)
335:             fired = True
336:     return text, fired
337: 
338: 
339: def canonical_key(text: str) -> str:
340:     """Aggressive key used for dictionary lookup (never shown to a user)."""
341:     t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
342:     t = "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c))
343:     t = t.lower()
344:     t = t.replace("’", "'").replace("‐", "-").replace("–", "-").replace("—", "-")
345:     t = re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "", t)
346:     return t
347: 
348: 
349: def clean_surface(raw: str) -> dict:
350:     """Clean one intervention string.
audit_evidence_claims\m12\root_checks\alias_http.py [(6, 'PORT=8918')]
